c0:["$","$L275",null,{"id":"ezquality---quality","children":[["$","code",null,{"children":"EZQuality"}]," - Quality"]}]
c1:["$","p",null,{"children":"Per-graph quality combo. JS overlays family-specific sampler, UNET, CLIP, and VAE widgets."}]
c2:["$","p",null,{"children":"!!! warning \"Lab notes\""}]
27e:T51c,Per-graph quality combo. presets.py is the only tier definition and the frontend reads a table generated from it. The combo lists only the tiers this graph's family can honor (presets.tiers_for_family) plus lab and custom, so a Klein graph never offers the FLUX 3 or Qwen-Image rows and a Wan graph never offers a weight swap it has no loader for; a graph with no sampler and no loader to write carries no combo at all. The combo is DERIVED from the widgets, not typed: it names the tier whose owned parameters the widgets actually hold, lab when they match the load-time authored values, custom otherwise, and it re-derives on load, graph change, widget edit, and Queue. custom is not absorbing - putting the owned widgets back on a tier's values re-derives that tier. A tier value that cannot be written (weight not installed, widget not built, a tier outside the family) is a FAILED apply: the combo drops to custom and the caption names what diverged, and the CLI states the refusal on stderr and writes the graph unchanged. Writes resolve per node instance, so every sampler and loader the family reaches in a graph is written. Only parameters the family's tiers own are compared, so a CFG edit on a steps-only family keeps the level. A combo whose node mode is nonzero is ignored.
